It mostly stays with work, I think. Like, the reason I even came in is I keep beating myself up that I’m not really senior material, you know? Like I somehow fooled everyone into thinking I can handle more than I actually can.

Then it turns into, like… I fooled everyone into thinking I can handle this job. So yeah, I do beat myself up about it even when there isn’t a real problem right then.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work-related concerns, do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried about other things in your daily life, like relationships, health, or other responsibilities?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not in the same way, I don’t think. I mean, there’s normal stuff, like bills or whether my car’s making that weird noise again—actually last month it was this little rattle and I convinced myself it was going to be some huge repair, but then it was nothing, just a loose thing.\n\nBut yeah, I’m not sure I’d say I’m worrying about everything all the time. It mostly seems to circle back to work, or at least that’s the thing I notice the most.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms, like feeling restless, having trouble relaxing, or muscle tension, especially when you're thinking about these work concerns?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t really think of it as, like, a body thing. Maybe I shift around in my chair sometimes, but I do that anyway—my desk chair is terrible, and I’ve been meaning to replace it, but then I start comparing chairs online and somehow end up reading reviews for standing desks.\n\nBut yeah… when I’m thinking about work, I mostly notice the thoughts, not so much muscle tension or anything. I haven’t really paid attention to whether my body’s doing something at the same time.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your worrying tends to stay focused on your work and performance, or does it often spread to other parts of your life, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: It mostly stays with work, I think. Like, the reason I even came in is I keep beating myself up that I’m not really senior material, you know? Like I somehow fooled everyone into thinking I can handle more than I actually can.\n\nAnd then I’ll be doing something totally unrelated, like trying to watch a show, and I’m not even following the plot because I’m replaying some ticket I handled or something I said in standup. Although, honestly, sometimes the show isn’t that good anyway—I started one the other night and the whole interface kept recommending things I’d already watched, which is annoying.\n\nBut yeah, sorry… it’s mostly that work-performance thing. Feeling like I don’t deserve the role or like I’m going to be found out.\n\nDoctor: When you have these thoughts about not being senior material or feeling like you’ve fooled people, do they feel intrusive or unwanted, and do you feel any need to do something—like repeatedly check your work or mentally replay conversations—to try to reduce the anxiety they bring?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. “Intrusive” sounds a little… I don’t know, dramatic maybe? They’re unwanted in the sense that I’d rather not be thinking about work when I’m trying to make dinner or whatever.\n\nAs far as needing to do something, I don’t really know. I mean, I do look back at stuff sometimes, but part of my job is checking things, so it’s hard to tell what’s normal and what’s me being weird about it. I mean… I guess not in the same way? The thing that made me come in is really this work thing—like I keep beating myself up that I’m not actually senior material, and that I somehow fooled everyone into thinking I can handle more than I can.

I don’t really trust my mood or my judgment lately, so it feels easier to just not be around people and risk saying the wrong thing or deciding something stupid.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or like you're not good enough, even when others don't seem to see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s kind of the main thing, honestly. Maybe I shift around in my chair sometimes, but I do that anyway—my desk chair is terrible, and I’ve been meaning to replace it, but then I start comparing chairs online and somehow end up reading reviews for standing desks.\n\nBut yeah… when I’m thinking about work, I mostly notice the thoughts, not so much muscle tension or anything. I haven’t really paid attention to whether my body’s doing something at the same time.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your worrying tends to stay focused on your work and performance, or does it often spread to other parts of your life, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: It mostly stays with work, I think. Like, the reason I even came in is I keep beating myself up that I’m not really senior material, you know? Like I somehow fooled everyone into thinking I can handle more than I actually can.\n\nAnd then I’ll be doing something totally unrelated, like trying to watch a show, and I’m not even following the plot because I’m replaying some ticket I handled or something I said in standup. Although, honestly, sometimes the show isn’t that good anyway—I started one the other night and the whole interface kept recommending things I’d already watched, which is annoying.\n\nBut yeah, sorry… it’s mostly that work-performance thing. Feeling like I don’t deserve the role or like I’m going to be found out.\n\nDoctor: When you have these thoughts about not being senior material or feeling like you’ve fooled people, do they feel intrusive or unwanted, and do you feel any need to do something—like repeatedly check your work or mentally replay conversations—to try to reduce the anxiety they bring?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. “Intrusive” sounds a little… I don’t know, dramatic maybe? I mean, I do look back at stuff sometimes, but part of my job is checking things, so it’s hard to tell what’s normal and what’s me being weird about it. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n11\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF40.00, F42.0, F32.0, F32.1, F33.0, F33.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I came in because I keep getting stuck on this idea that I’m not really, you know, senior material at work. Like I somehow convinced everyone I could handle more than I actually can. It’s stupid because people say I’m doing fine, but then I’ll be sitting there thinking, “No, I fooled them.” \n\nSorry, that sounds dramatic. I work with computer systems, and there’s always some new thing breaking or some vendor saying it’s not their fault—anyway, I just keep beating myself up over it, and it’s been hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried about your work performance most days, even when there isn’t a specific problem happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it is kind of most days. Not always because something actually went wrong, more like this background thing where I’m thinking, “They’re going to realize I’m not actually senior material.” \n\nIt’s funny, because I can be doing something normal, like checking a system log or answering an email, and then I’ll remember some tiny thing I said in a meeting and start thinking I sounded like I didn’t know what I was talking about. Then it turns into, like… I fooled everyone into thinking I can handle this job. So yeah, I do beat myself up about it even when there isn’t a real problem right then.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work-related concerns, do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried about other things in your daily life, like relationships, health, or other responsibilities?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not in the same way, I don’t think. I mean, there’s normal stuff, like bills or whether my car’s making that weird noise again—actually last month it was this little rattle and I convinced myself it was going to be some huge repair, but then it was nothing, just a loose thing.\n\nBut yeah, I’m not sure I’d say I’m worrying about everything all the time. It mostly seems to circle back to work, or at least that’s the thing I notice the most.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms, like feeling restless, having trouble relaxing, or muscle tension, especially when you're thinking about these work concerns?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t really think of it as, like, a body thing. Maybe I shift around in my chair sometimes, but I do that anyway—my desk chair is terrible, and I’ve been meaning to replace it, but then I start comparing chairs online and somehow end up reading reviews for standing desks.\n\nBut yeah… when I’m thinking about work, I mostly notice the thoughts, not so much muscle tension or anything. I haven’t really paid attention to whether my body’s doing something at the same time.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your worrying tends to stay focused on your work and performance, or does it often spread to other parts of your life, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: It mostly stays with work, I think. Like, the reason I even came in is I keep beating myself up that I’m not really senior material, you know? Like I somehow fooled everyone into thinking I can handle more than I actually can.\n\nAnd then I’ll be doing something totally unrelated, like trying to watch a show, and I’m not even following the plot because I’m replaying some ticket I handled or something I said in standup. Although, honestly, sometimes the show isn’t that good anyway—I started one the other night and the whole interface kept recommending things I’d already watched, which is annoying.\n\nBut yeah, sorry… it’s mostly that work-performance thing. Feeling like I don’t deserve the role or like I’m going to be found out.\n\nDoctor: When you have these thoughts about not being senior material or feeling like you’ve fooled people, do they feel intrusive or unwanted, and do you feel any need to do something—like repeatedly check your work or mentally replay conversations—to try to reduce the anxiety they bring?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. “Intrusive” sounds a little… I don’t know, dramatic maybe? They’re unwanted in the sense that I’d rather not be thinking about work when I’m trying to make dinner or whatever.\n\nAs far as needing to do something, I don’t really know. I mean, I do look back at stuff sometimes, but part of my job is checking things, so it’s hard to tell what’s normal and what’s me being weird about it. Like if a system change goes out, you’re supposed to verify it, and then there are logs, and then someone pings you about something unrelated and suddenly you’re in three windows and—sorry.\n\nI guess I haven’t really thought of it as “I have to do this to calm down.” It’s more like I get stuck on it in my head for a while.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about your work performance cause you a lot of distress or interfere with your ability to do things you normally enjoy or need to do in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a little. I mean, I’m still getting to work, so it’s not like I’ve completely fallen apart or anything. But I do find myself double-checking things more than I used to, like rereading something I already know I checked.\n\nAnd outside of work, I’ve kind of pulled back from people. Friends, family… not in some big dramatic way, just not answering as much or avoiding making plans. I don’t really trust my mood or my judgment lately, so it feels easier to just not be around people and risk saying the wrong thing or deciding something stupid.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or like you're not good enough, even when others don't seem to see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s kind of the main thing, honestly. I keep feeling like I’m not actually good enough for the senior role, like I somehow fooled people into thinking I can handle more than I really can.\n\nAnd the weird thing is, nobody’s really saying that to me. It’s more just in my own head. Like I’ll get normal feedback or someone will ask a question, and I’ll turn it into this whole thing about how they’re going to realize I shouldn’t be there. Sorry, I know I’m circling back to the work thing again, but yeah… that guilt or not-good-enough feeling is definitely there.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way about yourself—like you're not good enough or don’t deserve something—even in times when work wasn’t on your mind or before these concerns about your job started?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess not in the same way? The thing that made me come in is really this work thing—like I keep beating myself up that I’m not actually senior material, and that I somehow fooled everyone into thinking I can handle more than I can.\n\nI’ve had normal insecurity before, I think everybody does, like when you’re buying a car and wondering if you got ripped off—sorry, that’s not really the same thing. But this feels tied to the job and the senior role more than some general “I don’t deserve anything” thing. It’s more like I can’t get away from the idea that I’m not as capable as they think I am.\n\nDoctor: Over the last two weeks, have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most days, or lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days lately I’ve had this kind of heavy, low feeling. Not like I’m crying all day or anything, but more like I’m just moving through work and then going home and everything feels sort of muted, like the color’s turned down.\n\nI still do what I’m supposed to do, mostly, but it’s just… flat, I guess. Even stuff I’d normally look forward to doesn’t really feel like much right now.\n\nDoctor: Over the last two weeks, have you noticed a loss of energy or felt fatigued most days, even when you’re not doing anything physically or mentally demanding?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s not like I’m doing heavy labor or anything—I’m just at a desk most of the day—but by the time I get home from the office, even little things feel stupidly hard. Like checking the mail or loading the dishwasher feels like this whole production.\n\nAnd then I’ll stand there thinking, “Why am I making such a big deal out of plates?” I mean, my neighbor has three kids and somehow has energy to mow the lawn after work, so then I start comparing myself, which probably isn’t helpful. But yeah, the fatigue is there.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think that’s been happening. Like, normally I’d be looking forward to the weekend because I could grill something, or maybe go fishing if the weather’s decent, or just put on a record and kind of mess around the house.

Lately the grill stuff just sits there, the fishing gear’s in the corner, records are right there and I don’t even bother. It’s not that I suddenly hate any of it, it’s more like… I look at it and just don’t really care enough to start.

Although, honestly, sometimes the show isn’t that good anyway—I started one the other night and the whole interface kept recommending things I’d already watched, which is annoying.\n\nBut yeah, sorry… it’s mostly that work-performance thing. Feeling like I don’t deserve the role or like I’m going to be found out.\n\nDoctor: When you have these thoughts about not being senior material or feeling like you’ve fooled people, do they feel intrusive or unwanted, and do you feel any need to do something—like repeatedly check your work or mentally replay conversations—to try to reduce the anxiety they bring?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. “Intrusive” sounds a little… I don’t know, dramatic maybe? They’re unwanted in the sense that I’d rather not be thinking about work when I’m trying to make dinner or whatever.\n\nAs far as needing to do something, I don’t really know. I mean, I do look back at stuff sometimes, but part of my job is checking things, so it’s hard to tell what’s normal and what’s me being weird about it. Like if a system change goes out, you’re supposed to verify it, and then there are logs, and then someone pings you about something unrelated and suddenly you’re in three windows and—sorry.\n\nI guess I haven’t really thought of it as “I have to do this to calm down.” It’s more like I get stuck on it in my head for a while.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about your work performance cause you a lot of distress or interfere with your ability to do things you normally enjoy or need to do in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a little. I mean, I’m still getting to work, so it’s not like I’ve completely fallen apart or anything. But I do find myself double-checking things more than I used to, like rereading something I already know I checked.\n\nAnd outside of work, I’ve kind of pulled back from people. Friends, family… not in some big dramatic way, just not answering as much or avoiding making plans. I don’t really trust my mood or my judgment lately, so it feels easier to just not be around people and risk saying the wrong thing or deciding something stupid.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or like you're not good enough, even when others don't seem to see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s kind of the main thing, honestly. I keep feeling like I’m not actually good enough for the senior role, like I somehow fooled people into thinking I can handle more than I really can.\n\nAnd the weird thing is, nobody’s really saying that to me. It’s more just in my own head. Like I’ll get normal feedback or someone will ask a question, and I’ll turn it into this whole thing about how they’re going to realize I shouldn’t be there. Sorry, I know I’m circling back to the work thing again, but yeah… that guilt or not-good-enough feeling is definitely there.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way about yourself—like you're not good enough or don’t deserve something—even in times when work wasn’t on your mind or before these concerns about your job started?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess not in the same way? The thing that made me come in is really this work thing—like I keep beating myself up that I’m not actually senior material, and that I somehow fooled everyone into thinking I can handle more than I can.\n\nI’ve had normal insecurity before, I think everybody does, like when you’re buying a car and wondering if you got ripped off—sorry, that’s not really the same thing. But this feels tied to the job and the senior role more than some general “I don’t deserve anything” thing. It’s more like I can’t get away from the idea that I’m not as capable as they think I am.\n\nDoctor: Over the last two weeks, have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most days, or lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days lately I’ve had this kind of heavy, low feeling. Not like I’m crying all day or anything, but more like I’m just moving through work and then going home and everything feels sort of muted, like the color’s turned down.\n\nI still do what I’m supposed to do, mostly, but it’s just… flat, I guess. Even stuff I’d normally look forward to doesn’t really feel like much right now.\n\nDoctor: Over the last two weeks, have you noticed a loss of energy or felt fatigued most days, even when you’re not doing anything physically or mentally demanding?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s not like I’m doing heavy labor or anything—I’m just at a desk most of the day—but by the time I get home from the office, even little things feel stupidly hard. Like checking the mail or loading the dishwasher feels like this whole production.\n\nAnd then I’ll stand there thinking, “Why am I making such a big deal out of plates?” I mean, my neighbor has three kids and somehow has energy to mow the lawn after work, so then I start comparing myself, which probably isn’t helpful. But yeah, the fatigue is there.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends, don’t feel as rewarding or pleasurable as they used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s been happening. Like, normally I’d be looking forward to the weekend because I could grill something, or maybe go fishing if the weather’s decent, or just put on a record and kind of mess around the house.\n\nLately the grill stuff just sits there, the fishing gear’s in the corner, records are right there and I don’t even bother. It’s not that I suddenly hate any of it, it’s more like… I look at it and just don’t really care enough to start.\n\nDoctor: Over the last two weeks, have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight—like eating more or less than usual—even when you’re not trying to change your weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a little. My appetite’s been kind of weird—like I’ll get home and just… not really feel like making dinner, so I’ll grab a few bites of something or just skip supper.\n\nI noticed my belt’s a notch looser too, which is odd because I’m definitely not trying to lose weight. I mean, I used to be more regular about meals—my dad was always big on “sit down and eat a real dinner,” that kind of thing—but lately it’s more like I just don’t bother.\n\nDoctor: Over the last two weeks, have you lost interest in most things, even ones you used to really enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that’s the thing. It’s not like I can’t name stuff I like, you know? I usually look forward to grilling on a Saturday, or going fishing if I can get out early enough, or just putting on a record and doing whatever around the house.\n\nBut lately I just kind of… leave it all there. The grill stuff is sitting there, the fishing gear’s still in the corner, records are right by the stereo, and I look at it like, “Yeah, maybe later,” and then I don’t. I don’t really feel much pull toward any of it right now."
